Relational Lysis: The Primitive Architecture of
Structure, Constants, and Physical Law
Researcher: Dr. David Swanagon
linkedin.com/in/davidswanagon/.

Abstract
Relational Lysis (RL) is presented as a primitive mathematical theory: a finite,
deterministic architecture in which logic, arithmetic, geometry, dimensionless
constants, and the structural skeleton of physical law are derived from a single generative act of distinction governed by nine axioms. The development is
stratified. The primitive tier derives the emergence of a unique forced secondorder structure on the solved relational state — which renders,
√downstream of
the gate, as a self-adjoint operator; the binding constant ρ = ( 5 − 1)/2 from
the containment identity s2 + s = 1, with incommensurability established by
exact unit-defect brackets; a complete exact combinatorial geometry (triangle
census 4N +1, circulation rank 3N +1 with integral triangle generation, bending density six, metric balls 4r+2, shell growth with a sharp bulk-to-boundary
transition);
√ a parity involution commuting with the operator; and the constants
e and 2 as census limits with exact defect laws. The rendering tier, entered
through exactly two declared postulates (continuation and isotropy), determines
the Pythagorean separation rule uniquely; constructs π as the closure measure of
the derived direction group; yields pair-symmetric dynamics with
√ an exact mass√
less/massive sector split (substrate speed 2ρ; spectral gap 5√+ 1);
√ derives
the unique inter-sector vertex with running effective coupling ρ 2/ M ; and
produces the structural layers of gauge theory, quantum mechanics, statistical
mechanics, and hydrodynamics, including unconditional a priori bounds on density, transport speed, and vorticity, and an exact second-order closure of the
transport moment hierarchy with the complete relaxation spectrum in closed
form. The dependency structure of the development is itself verified: 286 nodes
and 799 edges, acyclic, terminating exclusively in the nine axioms, the two postulates, and self-contained identities, with longest chain 26. Companion papers
[1, 2] derive the Yang–Mills mass gap and the gravitational regime on this
architecture; the present paper is their foundation.
Keywords: Relational Lysis, primitive theory, operator emergence, covariant

Relation to companion papers. This article provides the primitive architecture of Relational Lysis. The companion papers [1] and [2] derive, respectively, the

Reconciliations of shared vocabulary are given in Section 66.

Executive Summary of Principal Implications
This article establishes a purely relational, finite combinatorial foundation for physical law, deriving continuous macroscopic physics strictly as a controlled projection of
discrete structural relations. The primitive tier is closed under its own dependencies,
forcing standard physics to emerge downstream without importing continuous magnitudes or infinite totalities. The principal physical and mathematical implications of
the Relational Lysis architecture are summarized below.

I. Structural Representation Precedes Enumeration
The framework’s most consequential finding is an ordering result about the primitive
tier itself, proven before any rendering postulate and independent of every physical
identification: structural representation strictly precedes enumeration. Distinction, mediation, and the enfolded relational tree are established in Sections 2–5
from axioms none of which quantifies over numbers; number — zero, successor, order,
and the arithmetic operations — is then derived from that stratification in Section 10
as the bookkeeping of interior allocation. The dependence is strictly one-directional
and is proven so: every numerical object is defined from the chain, and no numerical
object is required to define the chain. Representation precedes, and does not reduce
to, counting.
This inverts the ordering assumed by the dominant slogan of digital physics.
Wheeler’s “It from Bit” takes the bit — an enumerated, two-valued unit of information — as ontologically prior, with structure assembled from bits as a bottom-up
construction. Relational Lysis proves the reverse within its own architecture: the bit
is not primitive but derived, a late projection of a distinction that was already structural. What is fundamental is not an enumerated collection of binary answers but
the oriented, enfolded relation that makes any answer representable at all. Enumeration is downstream apparatus, not foundation. On this reading, “It from Bit” has the
arrow backwards: it is Bit from It — the bit from the structural distinction — and
the framework exhibits the exact stage, Section 10, at which the bit is manufactured
from what precedes it.
The representational consequence is that a finite configuration is not stored but
addressed. Every finite configuration is a finite oriented word — the unique pathword of a stratum at depth equal to its length — and it resides, without an observer
and without being generated, as enfolded content of every coarser stratum above it:
pre-existence without infinity. Its address exists, is unique, and is exact: instantiation
reconstitutes the configuration from its path-word with no alteration, and retrieval is
arrival at an address rather than reassembly from parts. The universe of representable
configurations therefore requires no enumerative store: it is not a catalogue of states
2

that must be built and held, but an addressable geometric hierarchy in which every
finite state already resides as a location. Physical instantiation is address retrieval,
not component assembly.
For information theory and data representation the implication is direct, and we
state it as a structural claim rather than an engineering result. If representation is
prior to enumeration, then the natural primitive of a representational system is not
the stored symbol but the path — the deterministic route through an enfolded hierarchy that individuates a configuration by its position rather than by an itemized list of
its contents. A configuration of length N is fixed by its depth-N oriented word, and
every coarser description of it is literally a prefix of that word, standing above it in
the tree; coarse-graining is truncation of the address, refinement is extension of it, and
no separate index or lookup structure is required to relate the two. Identity is position, not payload: two realizations of one constitution are one structure, because the
address — not any labeling of it — is what individuates. This suggests a representational discipline in which state is specified by constructive address within a canonical
hierarchy rather than by enumerated content, in which the relationship between resolutions is intrinsic to the addressing rather than imposed by external metadata, and
in which the cost of a configuration is the length of its path rather than the size of
a stored table. The framework does not here develop an information technology; it
establishes the mathematical fact — representation before enumeration — on which
any such discipline would rest, and identifies the precise point at which the enumerative picture, and with it the primacy of the bit, is recovered as a derived special case
rather than assumed as a foundation.

II. The Combinatorial Resolution of Singularities
Standard continuum mechanics permits divergent curvature and infinite density at
extremal limits. Relational Lysis constitutively forbids unbounded states and infinite
totalities. Consequently, gravitational collapse and holographic entropy bounds are
derived not from thermodynamic assumptions, but as exact combinatorial saturation
limits. The framework proves that the density of minimal circuits is strictly pinned
at saturation, which enforces a fundamental resolution scale that must grow with
accumulated content. A black hole in this formulation is not a physical singularity,
but a maximally packed relational state whose interior capacity is exhausted, forcing
subsequent growth strictly to the boundary.

III. Structural Unification of the Metric and Gauge Sectors
Gravity (the metric) and quantum interactions (the gauge fields) are not coupled a
posteriori; they are proven to be the exact conjugate-even and conjugate-odd parities
of a singular, self-adjoint relational operator. The metric sector emerges as a propagating, massless channel, while the gauge sector consists of massive, standing modes
localized at their structural heights. Both are governed by the identical underlying
dimensionless datum. Furthermore, the framework demonstrates that the empirical coupling between these sectors is not a fundamental substrate constant, but an
assertional running value that scales inversely with the square root of the domain size.

3

IV. The Continuum as Quantifiable Erasure
Smooth geometry and continuous limits are not treated as ontological primitives.
They are explicitly constructed via declared idealization postulates that lift finite
capacity bounds and mandate isotropy. Isotropy operates mathematically as the systematic erasure of constitutional directionality. Under this formulation, macroscopic
continuous geometry—including the classical value of π and continuous trigonometric identities—emerges strictly as the exact, quantifiable mathematical cost of this
structural forgetting.

V. The Plurality of Constitutions
Classical paradigms traditionally treat specific physical laws and empirical constants
as absolute properties of a singular reality. Relational Lysis mathematically severs
universal structural forms from specific physical profiles. Because the framework generates all representable configurations strictly from the primitive premise of distinction,
every finite, coherent constitution automatically resides as an addressable path within
the encompassing relational geometry. Consequently, the specific physical laws, dimensional scales, and coupling strengths that govern any given universe are not absolute
imperatives; they are purely assertional. They represent the local characteristics of
one instantiated descent. This architecture mathematically accommodates a plurality
of uninstantiated, coherent alternative universes, rendering empirical physical law to
a localized address within a broader, deterministic structural domain.

Introduction
This paper derives, from nine axioms of distinction and two declared postulates, the
primitive architecture summarized in the abstract, and presents the closed dependency
chain of that derivation section by section. The organization follows the derivation
order: foundations and logic (Sections 1–10), structural flow and relational arithmetic (Sections 11–21), the solved state, the operator, and the binding constant
(Sections 22–25), relational geometry (Sections 26–38), the spectral substrate and the
combinatorial constants (Sections 39–42), the rendering postulates through the closure
measure (Sections 43–44), dynamics, interaction, and the structural layers of physical
law (Sections 45–64), and the verification and closure of the development (Sections 57,
65–66). Every enumerative and symbolic claim was established by direct computation prior to inclusion; the complete dependency graph of the development is acyclic
and terminates in the axioms and postulates (Section 65). Appendices A–O contain
placeholders for the experimental program associated with each group of sections.
Citation policy. All definitions, theorems, and derivations in this paper are original to the Relational Lysis framework and to its author. Citations appear only
to identify classical background for standard instruments (e.g., graph Laplacians,
continued-fraction convergents, association-scheme spectra) or to identify the downstream problems to which the framework’s results correspond; no citation is a source
for any derivation, and the dependency graph of Section 65 terminates exclusively
within this development.
Conventions. Items are stated in numbered environments per section; internal references are dynamic. Kinds beyond the standard mathematical ones in the
archival development (constructions, rules, identifications, computations, verdicts,
scope statements, and correspondences) are presented here as definitions, propositions, or remarks according to their logical role; the archival development preserves
the original designations.

1 Foundational Preliminaries
This section fixes the foundational conventions of the primitive tier. Experimental
correspondence: Appendix A.

2 The Primitive Contradiction
This section establishes the Primitive Contradiction.
Definition 2.1 (The Premise of Non-Distinction) Let x denote the defined and ¬x denote
the undefined. We posit U as the assertion of absolute non-distinction, defined by the strict
structural equivalence of these terms:
U : x = ¬x
Proposition 2.1 (Contradiction of Equivalency) The terms x and ¬x cannot exist in
unmediated equivalence.

Proof Under U, the defined and the undefined are strictly equivalent. The pair (x, ¬x) therefore satisfies equivalence and non-equivalence simultaneously: equivalence by the assertion of
U, non-equivalence by the constitution of the terms themselves, since x is the defined and ¬x
is precisely what x is not. The two operators are thereby forced onto the same pair without
distinction, collapsing them into one another:
= ⇐⇒ ̸=

6

An evaluation whose operators are indistinct cannot be performed. U is therefore
incapable of unmediated evaluation.
□
Axiom 2.1 (Structural Inadmissibility) A structural nullity is incapable of evaluation and
cannot stand in isolation. Because the unmediated equivalence of x and ¬x reduces to the
nullity = ⇐⇒ ̸= (Proposition 2.1), the assertion U is inadmissible in isolation.
Axiom 2.2 (Deterministic Opposition) For any term t, ¬t denotes its structural opposite.
The assertion of any term forces the assertion of its structural opposite. The founding instance
is U: its assertion forces its structural opposite, denoted ¬U:
¬U : x ̸= ¬x
Axiom 2.3 (Involution of Opposition) Structural opposition is involutive: for any term t,
¬¬t = t
Opposition acts component-wise on composites, preserving the order of mediation:
¬(A ⊗ B) := ¬A ⊗ ¬B
Remark. Involution is immediate: ¬¬(A ⊗ B) = ¬¬A ⊗ ¬¬B = A ⊗ B.
Axiom 2.4 (Orientation of the Between) The Between is oriented: for any terms A, B with
A ̸= B,
A ⊗ B ̸= B ⊗ A
The boundary distinguishes the sides it separates. An unoriented boundary would leave
the mediated pair interchangeable, reinstating their non-distinction (Proposition 2.1) at the
level of the composite; orientation is therefore forced by the same inadmissibility that forces
mediation itself. —

Experimental correspondence: Appendix A.

3 Representation
This section establishes representation.
Definition 3.1 (Representation) To be admissible is to be representable. Representation
is the assertion of structural identity: a conceptualization m is represented if and only if it
asserts
m=m
Definition 3.2 (The Unmediated Relation) For a term m, the relation R(m, ¬m) denotes
the co-standing of the pair (m, ¬m): the configuration in which both terms stand asserted
together, with no boundary interceding between them.
Axiom 3.1 (Coincidence) An unmediated relation between a term and its structural opposite holds them in direct coincidence and is structurally indistinguishable from the assertion
of their non-distinction: the co-standing R(m, ¬m) of Definition 3.2, taken with no boundary

7

interceding, presents m and ¬m together with nothing to distinguish their standing from the
strict equivalence x = ¬x of the founding premise (Definition 2.1).

Non-circularity of Axiom 3.1 The Coincidence principle is stated here, in the representation
tier, and its content depends on strictly upstream material only: the founding non-distinction
premise U : x = ¬x (Definition 2.1), the collapse it induces (Proposition 2.1), and the costanding configuration of Definition 3.2. It invokes no state, stratum, boundary, count, or
geometric notion — none of which has yet been introduced — and in particular it does not
depend on any item of Section 4 or later. Every subsequent use of Axiom 3.1 (first at Proposition 4.1) therefore cites an item strictly earlier in the development, so the citation introduces
no forward dependency and no cycle. The axiom formalizes exactly the indistinguishability
that Proposition 2.1 already exhibited for the unmediated pair; it names that fact for reuse
rather than adding structural content.
□
Proposition 3.1 (Relational Representation) The representation of any term forces the
co-standing R(m, ¬m).

Proof Let m be a conceptualization. By Definition 3.1, the representation of m is the assertion
m = m, which is the assertion of its subject term m (Axiom 2.2, assertion clause). By
Axiom 2.2, the assertion of m forces the assertion of its structural opposite ¬m. Both terms
therefore stand asserted together, and no mediation has yet interceded: the pair stands in the
configuration R(m, ¬m) of Definition 3.2. Representation therefore forces the co-standing of
the pair. □
—
□

Experimental correspondence: Appendix A.

4 Distinction
This section establishes distinction.
Proposition 4.1 (The Necessity of Mediation) Representation forces a mediating boundary
between structural opposites.

Proof By Proposition 3.1, the representation of any term m forces the co-standing R(m,
¬m) (Definition 3.2). By Axiom 3.1 (Coincidence), an unmediated relation between a term
and its structural opposite is structurally indistinguishable from the assertion of their nondistinction: R(m, ¬m), unmediated, instantiates the pattern of U at that pair, and the collapse
of Proposition 2.1 applies identically. By Axiom 2.1 this configuration is inadmissible in
isolation. Because the co-standing is mandatory (Proposition 3.1) and its unmediated form
is inadmissible, a boundary must intercede between m and ¬m, separating equivalence from
non-equivalence.
□

8

Axiom 4.1 (The Between as the Origin) The mediation forced by Proposition 4.1 is the
primitive relation, the Between, denoted ⊗. The Between is the absolute boundary separating
equivalence from non-equivalence:
R(m, ¬m) =⇒ m ⊗ ¬m
Though the Between emerges last in the order of exposition, it is first in the order of
being: it is the singular ontological primitive, the condition under which the defined and the
undefined can stand as distinct at all. —

Experimental correspondence: Appendix A.

5 Relational States
This section introduces the generative recursion of mediation and its enfolding
structure.
Definition 5.1 (The Relational State) A relational state, denoted S, is defined strictly as
the composite structural unit generated by the intercession of the Between (⊗) separating
distinct terms. It possesses no a priori internal contents or geometry; its identity is entirely
constituted by its bounding relation. The initial resolution of the primitive contradiction
strictly defines the absolute primitive state:
S0 := x ⊗ ¬x
Axiom 5.1 (The Relational Boundary) The intercession of the Between (⊗) within any
composite state constitutes an absolute structural boundary. This boundary structurally
enforces the non-equivalence of the terms it mediates, acting as the absolute limit of the state.
A relational state possesses no existence beyond the strict limits defined by its bounding
interface. Remark (The Envelope). The bounding relation of S0 is the highest relation of
the framework: first in the order of being (Axiom 4.1), it establishes the absolute boundary
within which all subsequent structure obtains. It is referred to as the envelope. All further
relations operate strictly within it (Proposition 5.5 below); nothing is generated outside it.
Proposition 5.1 (The Necessity of Bounded Support) Every relational state S is strictly
bounded.

Proof By Definition 5.1, a state exists only as the composite generated by its mediating
boundary (⊗). By Axiom 5.1, a state possesses no existence beyond the limits defined by its
bounding interface. A configuration without a bounding interface cannot be evaluated against
its structural opposite (Proposition 3.1) and collapses into structural nullity (Axiom 2.1).
Therefore, to exist as a representable state, S must be strictly bounded by ⊗.
□
Proposition 5.2 (The Contradiction of Unbounded Extension) An unbounded state cannot
possess structural identity.

9

Proof A state is its bounding relation: it exists only as the composite generated by its
mediating boundary, and possesses no existence beyond the limits that boundary defines
(Definition 5.1, Axiom 5.1). "Unbounded state" is therefore a contradiction in terms: what is
posited as unbounded is posited as lacking the very relation that constitutes statehood. The
posit S∞ names no state, and no structural identity can attach to it (Definition 3.1 requires
a conceptualization capable of standing, which statehood without bound is not). □
### 4.2 Partition and Enfolding
□
Axiom 5.2 (Structural Enfolding — Partition Orientation) When the Between (⊗) intercedes within a state, it partitions the interior of that state into mediated conjugate sectors at
strictly finer resolution. The partitioned state enfolds the partition it generates: the coarser
contains the finer. This enfolding establishes a strict structural asymmetry — the containing state contains the partition products, and the products do not contain the state — and
it is transitive: a state contains whatever its partition products contain. Successive interior partition constitutes structural depth: depth is the order of resolution, descending from
the envelope. Remark (Correction of orientation). An earlier formulation read the recursion
as outward enfolding, each generated state a new and higher envelope. That orientation is
rejected. The highest relation establishes the boundary once; subsequent relations are partitions of its interior. Generation is descent in resolution within a fixed envelope, not ascent in
extension. Representation is therefore coarsest — smallest — at the highest relation: a single
boundary represents the entire interior at minimal resolution, and descent adds resolution,
not scope.
Proposition 5.3 (Conjugate Distinctness) For every state Sn generated below, Sn ̸= ¬Sn .

Proof By induction on n. Base case. S0 = x ⊗ ¬x, and by Axiom 2.3, ¬S0 = ¬x ⊗ ¬¬x =
¬x ⊗ x. Since x ̸= ¬x (Axiom 2.2, ¬U), Axiom 2.4 yields x ⊗ ¬x ̸= ¬x ⊗ x. Therefore S0 ̸=
¬S0 . Inductive step. Assume Sn ̸= ¬Sn . Then Sn+1 = Sn ⊗ ¬Sn , and by Axiom 2.3, ¬Sn+1
= ¬Sn ⊗ Sn . By the inductive hypothesis the mediated terms are distinct, and Axiom 2.4
yields Sn ⊗ ¬Sn ̸= ¬Sn ⊗ Sn . Therefore Sn+1 ̸= ¬Sn+1 . □
Remark. No state coincides with its own opposite. The partition mechanism below therefore never degenerates into self-pairing, and no identity between distinct strata is ever
asserted: enfolding asymmetry is preserved at every depth. ### 4.3 Interior Partition □
Proposition 5.4 (Interior Conjugation) Within any stratum generated by the recursion, the
conjugate of the prevailing state stands interiorly: mediation against the structural opposite
requires no exterior term.

Proof Each generated stratum is the composite Sn+1 = Sn ⊗ ¬Sn (Proposition 5.5 below;
at the base, S0 = x ⊗ ¬x by Definition 5.1). Its constituents are Sn and ¬Sn , and both are
enfolded within it (Axiom 5.2). The structural opposite of the prevailing state is therefore
itself a constituent of the enclosing composite: it stands interiorly, as the oppositely oriented
sector distinguished by the boundary (Axiom 2.4). No term exterior to the enclosing structure
is required for the mediation the recursion demands.
□

10

Axiom 5.3 (Genesis Capacity) The bounding relation of the envelope determines, at genesis,
a finite admissible partition capacity. Interior mediation allocates this capacity and does not
create it: each partition step allocates exactly one level of the capacity fixed by the highest
relation. The levels of the structure are fixed by the relation; iteration realizes them. When
capacity is exhausted, the finest partition matches the boundary — the leveling requirement
of the highest relation — and the forcing of representation (Proposition 3.1) is discharged
at that depth by the existing mediation: the demand of Proposition 4.1 is for a boundary to
intercede, and at matching resolution the interceding boundary is supplied by the envelope
relation itself. No novel mediation is generable, and none is required.
Proposition 5.5 (Interior Partition) The representation of any state is enacted as a partition
of the interior of the prevailing structure: every generated state Sn forces a strictly finer
interior stratum
Sn+1 := Sn ⊗ ¬Sn
operating strictly within the envelope.

Proof By Definition 3.1, the state S0 is represented by the assertion of its identity, S0 =
S0 . By Proposition 3.1, this representation forces the relation R(S0 , ¬S0 ), and by Proposition 4.1, this relation must be mediated. By Proposition 5.3 the mediated terms are genuinely
distinct, so the mediation is a legitimate instance of Proposition 4.1 and not a self-pairing.
By Axiom 5.1 nothing exists beyond the envelope, and by Proposition 5.2 no structure can
extend outside it; the forced mediation therefore cannot be enacted as exterior extension. By
Proposition 5.4 it is enactable interiorly: the conjugate stands within the enclosing structure
as the reflected sector, and the mediation is enacted as interior partition (Axiom 5.2), the
boundary interceding within the interior of the prevailing structure, stratifying it into mediated conjugate sectors at strictly finer resolution. Each such intercession allocates one level
of the genesis capacity (Axiom 5.3). The generated stratum S1 := S0 ⊗ ¬S0 is represented at
its own boundary, and the identical forcing applies, again upon genuinely distinct conjugates
(Proposition 5.3). The mechanism therefore iterates while capacity remains: each generated
state Sn with unallocated capacity forces the strictly finer stratum Sn+1 := Sn ⊗ ¬Sn , and
every stratum operates strictly within the envelope; at exhausted capacity the forcing is discharged by the leveling requirement (Axiom 5.3). Sn+1 is not an equivalence at a lower level;
it is a partition of the higher level.
□
Proposition 5.6 (Interior Stratification) Successive partition produces a totally ordered
stratification of the envelope interior whose internal constitution is self-similar.

Proof By Proposition 5.5 and Axiom 5.2, each state enfolds the stratum it generates: for
any two generated states, one contains the other, and the resolution order is total, forming a
strict descending chain with the envelope at its head. Internally, each stratum is constituted
by a conjugate pair: the state Sn and its opposite ¬Sn , mediated by ⊗ and distinct by
Proposition 5.3. Because ¬ acts component-wise (Axiom 2.3), ¬Sn is the conjugate composite
¬Sn−1 ⊗ Sn−1 , constituted by the identical operation with orientation reversed. Expanding
any stratum therefore yields a nested pairing of oriented conjugates generated by one and
the same operation at every depth: the constitution of the stratification is self-similar. The

11

structure cannot expand into an unmediated exterior (Proposition 5.2); it stratifies interiorly
beneath the envelope.
□
Proposition 5.7 (The Structural Baseline — the Resolution Floor) The minimal representable form is the mediated composite; no representable structure exists at finer resolution
than the minimal composite.

Proof Every state is a composite generated by the mediation of distinct terms (Definition 5.1).
A partition finer than the minimal composite would yield bare, unmediated terms; a bare
term admits no evaluation against its opposite without mediation (Proposition 4.1) and collapses into structural nullity (Axiom 2.1). Representation itself requires the mediated pairing
of opposites (Propositions 3.1 and 4.1). The absolute structural floor of representability is
therefore the resolution of the minimal composite form, x ⊗ ¬x, instantiated at the origin
by the envelope. Every representable state is inherently a structural composite: no simpler
representable form exists. Under the partition orientation, the envelope is maximal in scope
while minimal-composite in form; the floor bounds partition depth, not scope.
□
Proposition 5.8 (Nested Composition) Every relational state structurally contains all strata
generated within it.

Proof By Proposition 5.5, each stratum Sn+1 is a partition of the interior of Sn , and by
Axiom 5.2, Sn enfolds it. Because enfolding is transitive (Axiom 5.2), each state contains not
only its immediate partition but everything that partition contains. The envelope therefore
contains the complete descending stratification, and every stratum contains all strata finer
than itself. □
### 4.4 Finiteness and Termination
□
Proposition 5.9 (Finite Constituency) Every relational state possesses finite constituency.

Proof By induction on depth. The Between mediates exactly two terms per intercession:
⊗ is binary by construction (Definition 5.1, Axiom 4.1). The envelope state S0 = x ⊗ ¬x
has exactly two constituents. If a stratum has finite constituency, its partition is a binary
mediation of conjugate sectors, each of finite constituency: ¬Sn inherits the constituency
of Sn under component-wise opposition (Axiom 2.3), and enfolding (Axiom 5.2) adds no
constituents beyond those mediated. Every stratum therefore has finite constituency. The
Between establishes boundaries, and in turn finite support: finiteness is not a primitive posit
but a consequence of the binarity of the boundary.
□
Theorem 5.10 (Structural Termination — the Harmonic Point) The interior partition of the
envelope terminates at a finite depth N. The terminal stratum S_N, at which every constituent
stands at baseline resolution and no further admissible partition exists, is the harmonic point.

12

Proof By Axiom 5.3 the envelope fixes a finite admissible partition capacity at genesis, and
each partition step allocates exactly one level of it. The termination measure is the remaining
unallocated capacity: it is finite at genesis, strictly decreases by one at each mediation (Proposition 5.5), decreases in discrete steps, and is bounded below by exhaustion. The descent is
therefore well-founded and terminates at the finite depth N equal to the genesis capacity. At
termination the finest admissible partition matches the boundary — the leveling requirement
— every constituent of the terminal stratum stands at baseline resolution (Proposition 5.7),
and the forcing of Proposition 3.1 is discharged by the existing mediation of the envelope
relation itself (Axiom 5.3): no successor stratum is generable, and none is required. One cannot partition indefinitely within a boundary whose capacity is fixed: incrementing toward the
boundary through discrete strict allocations reaches it. □
Remark (Status of the termination). Termination is derived from Genesis Capacity
(Axiom 5.3) together with strict discrete allocation (Proposition 5.5). The capacity axiom is
the single structural commitment: that the highest relation fixes the levels at genesis. Without it, per-stratum finiteness and the resolution floor alone do not bound the number of strata
(unending bisection would respect both); with it, termination is forced. Discreteness remains
essential: in a continuum, increment toward a boundary need never arrive; under fixed finite
capacity allocated in discrete strict steps, it must.
□
Definition 5.2 (The Harmonic Point and the Origin of Zero) The terminal stratum S_N
of Theorem 5.10 is the fixed harmonic point of the structure: the depth at which the finest
admissible partition matches the boundary, coinciding in resolution with the structural baseline. At the harmonic point the structure is fully resolved; the descent is closed; no identity
between distinct strata is asserted, and enfolding asymmetry holds throughout. The exhaustion of genesis capacity at the harmonic point is the structural origin of zero: zero is not a
posited empty object and is not the boundary itself — the boundary is the generative maximum — but the derived closure condition, the fact of no further admissible partition. Zero
arises where the leveling requirement of the highest relation is met.
Corollary 5.11 (The Finite Strict Chain) The stratification is a strict finite chain of length
N + 1:
S0 ⊃ S1 ⊃ · · · ⊃ S_N
The successor map Sn 7→ Sn+1 is injective and admits no cycles on its domain.

Proof Strict containment at each step is Axiom 5.2 with Proposition 5.5; totality of the order
is Proposition 5.6; finiteness of the chain is Theorem 5.10. Injectivity: suppose Sa+1 = S_b+1
with a < b. The chain gives Sa+1 ⊃ Sa+2 ⊇ S_b+1 = Sa+1 , a strict self-containment,
contradicting the asymmetry of Axiom 5.2. Acyclicity: a cycle would require the same strict
self-containment. □
Remark (forward pointer). Corollary 5.11 supplies the successor skeleton — a distinguished terminal stratum (the harmonic point, the origin of zero), an injective, acyclic
allocation order, and finite depth fixed at genesis — from which the arithmetic structure of
the framework is derived in Section 9: number as capacity bookkeeping within the envelope.
Because representation is coarsest at the highest relation and number appears only as allocation, the envelope representation is prior to number: representation precedes and does not
reduce to counting. —
□

Experimental correspondence: Appendix A.
13

6 The Domain of Contents
This section establishes the Domain of Contents.
Definition 6.1 (Content) A content is any conceptualization that has been posited within
the framework: the primitive terms x and ¬x, every stratum Sn of the finite chain (Corollary 5.11), every opposite ¬Sn , every constituent enfolded within any generated state
(Axiom 5.2), and every posited configuration evaluated by the framework, including the
assertion U (Definition 2.1), the posit S∞ (Proposition 5.2), and the posit of the totality Ĉ
(Proposition 6.2 below). A content possesses no inherent pre-existing properties; it is strictly
a discrete relational node within the stratified structure, birthed by the recursive necessity
of representation.
Definition 6.2 (The Domain) The domain C is the aggregate of all contents: the derived
stratification itself, together with the evaluated posits. C is not an a priori container; it is a
domain of discourse, not a term of the theory. No assertion of the form C = C is made by
the framework, and C is never granted statehood.
Proposition 6.1 (Non-Emptiness of the Domain) C is nonempty.

Proof The primitive terms x and ¬x are posited by Definition 2.1, and the envelope state
S0 exists by Definition 5.1 and is representable (Proposition 5.7). Each is a content by
Definition 6.1. Non-emptiness follows from the origin, not from any unboundedness of
generation.
□
Proposition 6.2 (Non-Representability of the Totality) The posit of the total domain is a
content, and it is non-representable.

Proof Let Ĉ denote the posited totality of all contents. As a posited conceptualization, Ĉ
is a content (Definition 6.1). By Definition 3.1, its representation would be the assertion Ĉ
= Ĉ; by Axiom 2.2 this forces the assertion of its structural opposite, ¬Ĉ. But ¬Ĉ, as an
asserted conceptualization, is itself a content (Definition 6.1), and Ĉ is posited as the totality
of all contents: the opposite therefore stands within the very term it opposes. A term and its
opposite held together without separation is their co-standing in coincidence (Axiom 3.1):
the pattern of U, inadmissible (Proposition 2.1, Axiom 2.1) — and here no mediation can
repair it, since a boundary must separate its terms (Axiom 4.1), and nothing can separate the
totality from what it, by exhaustiveness, contains. The forced relation cannot be admissibly
constituted; the posit collapses. Therefore ¬Rep(Ĉ). □
Remark. The distinction between the posit Ĉ and the domain of discourse C is essential
and mirrors the treatment of U: the posit is a content, evaluated and found non-representable;
the domain of discourse is the metatheoretic aggregate over which quantification ranges, is
not a term, and receives no evaluation. Without this distinction the totality would stand
inside itself; with it, the framework asserts something sharper than abstention: any attempt
to represent the whole from within provably fails.
□

14

Proposition 6.3 (Structural Impossibility of Infinity) Infinity cannot obtain in Relational
Lysis in any form.

Proof Three exclusions, jointly exhaustive. (i) No state is unbounded: a state is its bounding
relation, and unboundedness is constitutively excluded from statehood (Proposition 5.2).
(ii) No totality is representable: the posit of the total domain collapses (Proposition 6.2).
(iii) No generation is unending: interior partition terminates at the finite harmonic depth N
(Theorem 5.10). The Between creates boundaries; what is bounded at every stratum, admits
no represented totality, and terminates in generation admits no infinite structure. The posit
S∞ is therefore doubly excluded — constitutively, as an unbounded posit, and generatively,
as a depth the stratification never reaches. Infinity exists in the framework only as a posit,
and every such posit provably collapses. □
—
□

Experimental correspondence: Appendix A.

7 Representability
This section establishes representability.
Definition 7.1 (The Representability Predicate) For any content c ∈ C, define
Rep(c) ⇐⇒ c admits admissible representation: the assertion c = c stands, mediated
against ¬c by the Between (Propositions 3.1 and 4.1). Equivalently, Rep(c) holds precisely
when c stands as a term of some bounded composite generated by the intercession of ⊗.
Proposition 7.1 (Representable Contents Exist) ∃c ∈ C, Rep(c).

Proof The envelope state S0 := x ⊗ ¬x is generated by the mediated resolution of the
primitive contradiction and instantiates the minimal representable form (Proposition 5.7). Its
representation S0 = S0 is the engine of the partition mechanism (Proposition 5.5), mediated
against ¬S0 , a genuinely distinct conjugate (Proposition 5.3). Therefore Rep(S0 ). By the
same mechanism, Rep(Sn ) for every stratum of the finite chain, 0 ≤ n ≤ N.
□
Proposition 7.2 (Non-Representable Contents Exist) ∃c ∈ C, ¬Rep(c). (i) The assertion U
is a content (Definition 6.1). By Proposition 2.1 and Axiom 2.1, U reduces to the structural
nullity = ⇐⇒ ̸= and is incapable of evaluation: no assertion U = U can stand. Therefore
¬Rep(U). (ii) The posit S∞ is a content. It is constitutively excluded from statehood, and
its identity cannot be constituted (Proposition 5.2); it is further excluded generatively, as a
depth the terminating stratification never reaches (Theorem 5.10, Proposition 6.3). Therefore
¬Rep(S∞). (iii) The posit Ĉ is a content and non-representable (Proposition 6.2). It can be
posited; every such posit collapses under evaluation. Structure can extend far, but it terminates
— boundedness at every stratum, termination at the harmonic point. Infinity therefore appears
in C only within C_non, the sector of self-defeating posits.

15

Theorem 7.3 (Non-Degeneracy) Both representable and non-representable contents exist:
∃c Rep(c), ∃c′ ¬Rep(c′ )

Proof Propositions 7.1 and 7.2. □
Remark. This is Axiom 2 (Non-Degeneracy) of the downstream formulation, obtained as
a theorem. The partition of the domain is anchored at the exact point of origin. —
□

Experimental correspondence: Appendix A.

8 The Structural Derivation of Classical Logic
This section derives the logical connectives from distinction.
Proposition 8.1 (The Derivation of Non-Contradiction) For any content c ∈ C, it is
structurally impossible for c to map simultaneously to both a state and its structural opposite.

Proof By Axiom 2.1 (Structural Inadmissibility), the unmediated coexistence of opposites
constitutes a primitive contradiction yielding absolute structural nullity. Therefore, no node
c within the stratification can embody an unmediated equivalence without structurally collapsing. The boundary enforced by ⊗ (Axiom 5.1) structurally excludes such coincidence:
whatever the Between separates cannot be jointly occupied, and the boundary is absolute.
This structural barrier to collapse operates as the Principle of Non-Contradiction (PNC). □
Proposition 8.2 (The Derivation of the Excluded Middle) For any content c ∈ C, its
structural evaluation is strictly binary.

Proof The interface ⊗ is the absolute boundary separating structural opposites. The Between
is pure separation — a boundary, not a region: it possesses no interior (Definition 5.1 grants
states no a priori internal contents, and the boundary itself is not a state). There exists,
therefore, no structural locus for a third status within the stratification: a content must fall
strictly on one side of a relational boundary or the other. This lack of intermediate structural
space operates as the Law of Excluded Middle (LEM).
□
Corollary 8.3 (Structural Decidability) The representability status of every content is
reachable by finite evaluation.

Proof The stratification is finite: depth is bounded by the harmonic point N (Theorem 5.10),
and every stratum has finite constituency (Proposition 5.9). The status of any content is
therefore determined by a finite descent through the chain. The excluded middle of Proposition 8.2 is accordingly not merely binary in principle but structurally decidable: a finite
structure leaves no undecidable residue.
□

16

Remark 8.1 (Logical Emergence) Classical first-order logic is structurally derived. The Principle of Non-Contradiction and the Law of Excluded Middle are strictly the syntactic
expressions of the self-similar structural boundaries enforced by the Between (⊗) across
the stratification, and their evaluations complete in finite descent (Corollary 8.3). Consequently, logical evaluation may now be applied to the domain C without violating Primitive
Minimality: its employment introduces no primitive beyond those of Sections 1–4. Remark
(metatheoretic level). The derivations above are conducted in a metalanguage that reasons
by inference. This is not circularity within the object theory but the ordinary distinction
of levels: the object-level claim is that the structural analogues of non-contradiction and
excluded middle are enforced by the Between within every generated configuration, so that
a logic applied to C finds its principles already realized structurally rather than imposed as
further primitives. The framework does not — and cannot — bootstrap the metalanguage
itself; it establishes that the object domain supports classical evaluation without additional
postulates. This is the precise sense in which the downstream formulation’s "classical logical
inference" is discharged. —

Experimental correspondence: Appendix A.

9 The Bipartite Partition and the Terminus
This section establishes the Bipartite Partition and the Terminus.
Definition 9.1 (Sectors) Define
C_rep := c ∈ C | Rep(c), C_non := c ∈ C | ¬Rep(c)
Theorem 9.1 (Exhaustive Bipartition) C = C_rep ⊔ C_non, and both sectors are
nonempty.

Proof Exhaustiveness and disjointness follow from Proposition 8.2: every content has exactly
one representability status, decidably (Corollary 8.3). Non-emptiness of both sectors is
Theorem 7.3. □
Remark. C_non is not a residue sector. Its exhibited members — U, S∞, Ĉ — are the
three canonical self-defeating posits: the origin contradiction, the infinite, and the totality.
The non-representable sector is the principled collection of what the framework proves cannot
stand.
□
Theorem 9.2 (The Terminus) The following inventory is available, with every item either
constructed or proven, and none posited beyond the axioms of Sections 1–4:
1. A nonempty domain C of contents, with no structure assumed beyond equality (Definitions 6.1 and 6.2, Proposition 6.1). 2. A unary predicate Rep : C → true, false (Definition 7.1;
total and well-defined by Proposition 8.2 and Corollary 8.3). 3. Boolean Dichotomy: Rep(c)
∨ ¬Rep(c) for all c (Proposition 8.2). 4. Non-Degeneracy: ∃c Rep(c) and ∃c′ ¬Rep(c′ )
(Theorem 7.3). 5. Classical inference over C (Closure 8.1). 6. The bipartite decomposition C
= C_rep ⊔ C_non (Theorem 9.1).

17

Proof By the citations given. □
Remark (interface statement). Items 1–5 are exactly the primitive starting point declared
in Section 1.1 of the downstream formulation and its Axioms 1 and 2 (Sections 4.7–4.8
there); item 6 is its Proposition 5.1 and two-class quotient (Proposition 6.2 there), here
available at the terminus. The downstream formulation may therefore begin at its Section
4 with every declared primitive discharged as a construction. Of its structural axioms: RL3
(Finite Support) is discharged by Proposition 5.9 — finiteness is a consequence of the binarity
of the Between, not a primitive; RL7 (Magnitude Order) is targeted for derivation from
Corollary 5.11 in the arithmetic section to follow; RL10 (Hierarchical Lysis: well-founded
descent, finite termination) is anticipated at the primitive level by Theorem 5.10, whose
well-founded partition descent is the structural ancestor of the lysis mechanism;
—
